On subjects where the relevant disagreements are about values, taste, or interpretation, the format misfits — the second vote will not converge because there is no correct answer to converge on.
- **The two-vote ritual without the discussion.** Skipping the pair-instruction step (just running two votes ten minutes apart) produces drift, not learning. The method requires the discussion.
- **The method as performance.** Some instructors run Peer Instruction *as theater* — the format is followed but the instructor explains immediately after the first vote, defeating the design. The discipline (instructor restraint) is the hard part.

## The lesson in one sentence

*A ConcepTest is an artifact whose pedagogical effect is the act of answering it under public commitment, learning from peers who answered differently, and seeing the room's convergence — the question is the lesson, and the explanation is what arrives only after the room has done the work.*

<!-- END: references/tear-downs/36-mazur-peer-instruction.md -->

---


<!-- BEGIN: references/tear-downs/README.md -->

# Tear-Downs

Deconstructions of specific apex artifacts. For each: what it is, what craft moves it deploys, why they work, what to steal.

These are for **calibration**. Read them before starting an artifact in a similar genre. Identify a move; deploy it; cite the debt in your own mind.

## Index

### Original ten

1. **Bartosz Ciechanowski — "GPS"** (`01-ciechanowski-gps.md`) — interactive explainer, scroll-driven, hand-crafted SVG
2. **Nicky Case — "The Evolution of Trust"** (`02-case-trust.md`) — explorable game as explainer
3. **Minard — "Napoleon's March" (1869)** (`03-minard-napoleon.md`) — data visualization as argument, multi-variable on one plane
4. **Distill.pub — "Attention and Augmented RNNs"** (`04-distill-attention.md`) — academic publication with interactive figures
5. **The Pudding — "How Well Can You Draw the Letter G?"** (`05-pudding-letter-g.md`) — participatory data journalism
6. **Linear — product UI** (`06-linear-product.md`) — interaction craft in a commercial product
7. **Neurath / Arntz — Isotype (1920s–40s)** (`07-neurath-isotype.md`) — pictorial information design, repetition for quantity
8. **Quanta Magazine** (`08-quanta-magazine.md`) — science journalism that respects its reader
9. **Stripe Documentation** (`09-stripe-docs.md`) — three-column technical documentation exemplar
10. **Sebastian Lague — Coding Adventures** (`10-sebastian-lague.md`) — honest technical exploration as content

### Diversification (Wave 1 expansion)

11. **Massimo Vignelli — NYC MTA Subway Map (1972)** (`11-vignelli-mta.md`) — network topology over geography; design-system-across-surfaces; the public-reaction-then-reevaluation story
12. **Marie Neurath — Isotype Children's Books (1948–86)** (`12-marie-neurath-children.md`) — Isotype tuned for child cognition; the transformer method; respect-as-default register
13. **Edward Tufte — *Visual Display* book as object (1983)** (`13-tufte-visual-display.md`) — generous margins as sidenote home; figures at column scale; self-publication as authorial control
14. **NYT — "Snow Fall: Avalanche at Tunnel Creek" (2012)** (`14-nyt-snowfall.md`) — full-bleed media as section break; print-derived typography in web frame; multi-author production model
15. **Derek Sivers — Personal Resume** (`15-derek-sivers-resume.md`) — text-only as confidence; first-person voice; convention-refusal as statement (Frank Chimero portfolio as alternate)
16. **GOV.UK — Self Assessment service** (`16-govuk-self-assessment.md`) — one question per page; plain English at scale; design-system-as-public-good

### Sci/med specialty

17. **Frank Netter — Atlas Plate 235 ("Heart In Situ")** (`17-netter-atlas-plate.md`) — layered overlay; rigid vascular color convention; anatomy-as-portrait register
18. **Kurzgesagt — In a Nutshell** (`18-kurzgesagt.md`) — flat-vector cosmic palette; recurring mascots as continuity; templated five-act narrative arc
19. **Pathoma — Fundamentals of Pathology** (`19-pathoma-condensed-review.md`) — condensed-review register; mnemonic-integrated bullets; deliberate-sacrifice discipline
20. **LIGO — GW150914 Figure 1 (*PRL* 2016)** (`20-ligo-gw150914.md`) — composite multi-panel with shared time axis; data + template + residual; annotation as argument

### Calibration breadth

21. **The Pudding — "Human Terrain"** (`21-pudding-on-science.md`) — data-essay scrollytelling; question-as-headline; deferred argument; table-fallback (distinct from Snow Fall multimedia register)
22. **Carl Sagan — "Pale Blue Dot" (1994)** (`22-sagan-pale-blue-dot.md`) — zoom-out as rhetorical structure; one image carrying entire argument; awe anchored in the concrete (paired with Gawande counterpoint)
23. **NHS — "Be Clear on Cancer" (bowel cancer phases 2012–19)** (`23-public-health-multi-surface.md`) — one message, four surfaces; action-orientation; pre/post measured behavior change
24. **Ernst Haeckel — *Kunstformen*, Plate 71** (`24-haeckel-kunstformen.md`) — symmetry as composition; specimen-grid layout; the productive *and* the corrupting case of aesthetic ambition over data
25. **Stripe Press — *Working in Public* (2020)** (`25-stripe-press-book.md`) — contemporary high-design publishing; type discipline at scale; web-to-print continuity; "Phaidon-for-software" aesthetic
26. **Bartosz Ciechanowski — *Mechanical Watch* (2022)** (`26-ciechanowski-mechanical-watch.md`) — *parameter persistence across ~30 hand-drawn SVG figures*; same-mechanism-from-multiple-angles register; the move that makes Watch beat *GPS* (the earlier Ciechanowski piece torn down at #01); paired with the new `scrollytelling.md` playbook

### Wave 9 expansion — ceiling synthesis (10 entries)

27. **NYT — "How the Virus Got Out" (2020)** (`27-nyt-virus-got-out.md`) — *camera-on-rails cinematographic scrollytelling*; scroll as choreographed camera path; hard cuts between geographic scales; distinct from #14 NYT *Snow Fall* (multimedia journalism) and #21 Pudding *Human Terrain* (data-essay restraint)
28. **NYT — Live Presidential Forecast ("the Needle") (2016, 2018, 2020)** (`28-nyt-election-needle.md`) — *animation as uncertainty channel*; jitter amplitude as live posterior variance; the unique axis no other entry teaches
29. **Bret Victor — "Up and Down the Ladder of Abstraction" (2011)** (`29-bret-victor-ladder.md`) — *the canonical explorable explanation*; scrub-time → scrub-parameter → see-the-whole-space; the lineage source for #01 and #26 Ciechanowski, and for the new `explorable-explanation.md` playbook
30. **David Goodsell — *Machinery of Life* / *Molecule of the Month* (1992–present)** (`30-goodsell-machinery-of-life.md`) — *scientific illustration as painting at molecular scale*; ink-outline-plus-flat-fill for molecular crowding at correct concentration; distinct from #17 Netter (human-scale anatomical line) and #24 Haeckel (specimen-grid)
31. **Massimo Vignelli — American Airlines Identity Standards Manual (1967)** (`31-vignelli-american-airlines.md`) — *identity-system as deliverable* (vs. #11 Vignelli MTA where the artifact is the map's application); the multi-page governance manual is the work product; the canonical exemplar for wow-taxonomy move 1.13
32. **Andreas Vesalius — *De humani corporis fabrica* (1543)** (`32-vesalius-fabrica.md`) — *founding scientific work as bound artifact with cultural-philosophical weight*; chiaroscuro woodcut at production ceiling; pose-as-living-while-dissected; distinct from #17 Netter (modern clinical) and #24 Haeckel (specimen-as-art)
33. **Anne Carson — *Nox* (2010)** (`33-anne-carson-nox.md`) — *book-as-physical-object where the form is grief itself*; accordion-fold, facsimile fidelity, lacuna as element; the Literary register's most extreme test case; distinct from #13 Tufte and #25 Stripe Press (book-as-object at other registers)
34. **Patricia Lockwood — *Priestdaddy* (2017) and *No One Is Talking About This* (2021)** (`34-lockwood-priestdaddy.md`) — *very-online register adapted to literary prose*; image-as-syntax-unit; recursive joke; the contemporary mutation of the Literary register documented in `editorial-voice.md`
35. **Andy Matuschak & Michael Nielsen — *Quantum Country* (2019)** (`35-quantum-country.md`) — *the mnemonic medium*; essay-as-card-engine; section-anchor return links; spaced-repetition cards interleaved at the encoding moment; the canonical implementation of the new `mnemonic-medium.md` playbook
36. **Eric Mazur — *Peer Instruction* and ConcepTests (1991–present)** (`36-mazur-peer-instruction.md`) — *assessment-as-instruction*; commit-before-reveal; pair-discussion-then-revote; distractors as diagnostic misconceptions; the canonical apex of the form `assessment-artifact.md` distinguishes from instructional artifacts

## Structure of each tear-down

- **What it is** — one paragraph
- **Why it matters** — what makes this apex
- **Moves deployed** — specific, named craft decisions
- **What to steal** — the one or two things worth lifting for your own work
- **Where to apply** — genres of artifact this thinking helps

## How to read these

- Don't imitate wholesale. Extract the *principle*, not the styling.
- Stealing is good. Homage with attribution is good. "Inspired by Ciechanowski's scroll-bound state" is an honest move.
- Study the whole piece before the tear-down. The tear-down is a map, not the territory.

## How to extend

Add a tear-down when:
- You encounter an artifact that genuinely surprised you with craft.
- You can name three specific moves in it and articulate why each works.
- It's publicly accessible (or widely archived).
- **It adds a calibration axis the library does not already cover.** Diversification across *domain* (engineering / medicine / journalism / civic / commercial / scientific), *region* (Western / non-Western / global-translated), *era* (pre-modern / mid-century / contemporary), *medium* (printed book / web interactive / scrollytelling / video / multi-surface campaign), and *author identity* (institutional / solo / hybrid; the gender, regional, and demographic spread of the makers) is an explicit curation goal. A new tear-down should make the platter *broader*, not deeper in an axis already well-covered.

Decline when:
- "It's good" is the only reason — tear-downs require articulation.
- You're imitating a signature rather than extracting a principle.
- The artifact duplicates an axis already represented. (A second American mid-century subway map after Vignelli; a second high-end Western anatomy atlas after Netter; a third *NYT*-style scrollytelling piece.) Diminishing returns set in fast.

## The cap, revised

The library originally capped at 25 (10 originals + 15 expansion). Wave 7 reopened the cap to admit entry #26 (Ciechanowski's *Mechanical Watch*, 2022) because the parameter-persistence-across-figures move it dissects is genuinely distinct from the earlier *GPS* tear-down (#01). Wave 9 admitted ten more (#27–#36) from a ceiling synthesis across interactive-figure, data/math, static-visual, long-form/narrative, and teaching/assessment clusters — each entry was vetted against the existing axes, and one candidate (Distill's *Building Blocks of Interpretability*) was declined as redundant against #04.

The principle is unchanged: *each tear-down must add a distinct calibration axis*. The number is a consequence of axis-coverage, not a budget. Future entries are admitted only when they earn a new axis, not when they elaborate an existing one. The discipline matters more, not less, as the library grows: by entry #50 the axis-coverage check should be *stricter* than at entry #26, because the ground already covered is wider and the remaining gaps are narrower.

The 36 currently catalogued axes span:

**Interactive and computational:**
- *Interactive technical explainer* (#01 Ciechanowski GPS, #02 Case, #04 Distill, #10 Lague)
- *Explorable explanation as fluid ladder-traversal* (#29 Bret Victor)
- *Same-mechanism-from-multiple-angles with shared state* (#26 Ciechanowski *Watch*)
- *The mnemonic medium — essay as card engine* (#35 *Quantum Country*)

**Data, visualization, and rhetoric:**
- *Data visualization as argument* (#03 Minard, #20 LIGO, #05 Pudding letter G)
- *Camera-on-rails cinematographic scrollytelling* (#27 NYT *Virus*)
- *Animation as uncertainty channel* (#28 NYT election needle)
- *Data-essay scrollytelling register* (#21 Pudding *Human Terrain*)

**Information design and systems:**
- *Information design as ontology* (#07 Neurath, #11 Vignelli MTA, #12 Marie Neurath)
- *Identity-system as deliverable* (#31 Vignelli American Airlines manual)
- *Civic / institutional service design* (#16 GOV.UK)
- *Multi-surface behavior-change campaign* (#23 NHS Be Clear on Cancer)

**Long-form journalism and prose:**
- *Long-form web journalism / multimedia register* (#14 NYT Snowfall)
- *Science journalism that respects the reader* (#08 Quanta)
- *Technical documentation* (#09 Stripe Docs)
- *Wonder register at peak* (#22 Sagan + Gawande counterpoint)
- *Very-online register in literary prose* (#34 Lockwood)
- *Book-as-physical-object where the form is grief* (#33 Anne Carson *Nox*)

**Book-as-object and printed work:**
- *Book-as-object at academic register* (#13 Tufte)
- *Contemporary high-design publishing* (#25 Stripe Press)
- *Founding scientific work as bound artifact with cultural-philosophical weight* (#32 Vesalius *Fabrica*)

**Scientific illustration:**
- *Anatomical illustration apex (human scale, clinical)* (#17 Netter)
- *Scientific illustration as painting at molecular scale* (#30 Goodsell)
- *Specimen-art with rigor/aesthetic tension* (#24 Haeckel)

**Product, UI, video:**
- *Product UI craft* (#06 Linear)
- *Explainer video studio system* (#18 Kurzgesagt)
- *Personal artifact / convention refusal* (#15 Sivers)

**Education and assessment:**
- *Condensed-review register* (#19 Pathoma)
- *Assessment-as-instruction with commit-before-reveal* (#36 Mazur ConcepTests)

**Wave 9's ten entries added the following genuinely new axes** to that list (one per entry): cinematographic scroll grammar (#27); animation-as-uncertainty (#28); explorable-explanation canon (#29); painterly scientific illustration at molecular scale (#30); identity-system-as-deliverable (#31); founding-scientific-work-as-bound-philosophical-artifact (#32); book-as-physical-object-where-form-is-grief (#33); very-online register in literary prose (#34); the mnemonic medium (#35); assessment-as-instruction (#36).

Past these axes, the marginal axis starts repeating fast — a new subway map adds little after Vignelli, a new anatomy atlas adds little after Netter, a new explorable explanation adds little after Victor and Ciechanowski, a new scrollytelling piece adds little after Snowfall / Pudding / NYT *Virus*. **The discipline is the axis check, not the number.** The library exists to *cover the calibration ground broadly*; once the ground is covered, additional entries dilute the reader's ability to use the library as a map.

If a candidate tear-down doesn't comfortably name a *new* axis, the answer is usually: study the existing entry on the closest axis instead, and bring the candidate's craft moves *into your work* without adding it to the library.

<!-- BEGIN: references/tear-downs/acc-aha-hf-2022-algorithm.md -->

# Tear-Down: 2022 ACC/AHA/HFSA HFrEF Treatment Algorithm (Heidenreich et al.)

**Originating bodies:** American College of Cardiology (ACC), American Heart Association (AHA), Heart Failure Society of America (HFSA)
**Canonical citation:** Heidenreich PA, Bozkurt B, Aguilar D, et al. "2022 AHA/ACC/HFSA Guideline for the Management of Heart Failure: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Clinical Practice Guidelines." *Circulation.* 2022;145(18):e895-e1032. doi:10.1161/CIR.0000000000001063
**Chosen artifact within the guideline:** Figure 11 (HFrEF Treatment Algorithm) and the associated companion tables on Class of Recommendation × Level of Evidence (Class I-III, LoE A / B-R / B-NR / C-LD / C-EO)
**Where to study:** the full guideline (open-access at the ACC website); the 2017 ACC Expert Consensus Decision Pathway for Optimization of Heart Failure Treatment as the algorithmic precursor; the European Society of Cardiology 2021 HF Guideline (McDonagh et al., *Eur Heart J*) as the contemporaneous parallel artifact
**Audience:** cardiologists, internists, hospitalists, advanced-practice providers managing heart failure; the algorithm is referenced in board examinations (ABIM Cardiovascular Disease) and in institutional quality metrics

## What it is

The HFrEF treatment algorithm is the single most-cited figure from the 2022 ACC/AHA/HFSA Heart Failure Guideline — a one-page decision-walking image that compresses the guideline's ~200 pages of evidence into an actionable pathway for treating heart failure with reduced ejection fraction (EF ≤ 40%). The algorithm names the **four pillars** of guideline-directed medical therapy (GDMT) — an ARNI (or, if ARNI not tolerated, an ACE inhibitor or ARB), a beta-blocker, a mineralocorticoid receptor antagonist (MRA), and an SGLT2 inhibitor — and directs the clinician to initiate all four within four to six weeks of diagnosis. Each recommendation node carries a **Class of Recommendation** annotation (Class I = strong, IIa = moderate, IIb = weak, III = harm/no benefit) and a **Level of Evidence** annotation (A, B-R, B-NR, C-LD, C-EO). The algorithm shows bidirectional escalation and de-escalation arrows, starter and target doses for each agent, and contingency branches for the most common intolerances. The whole pathway fits on one page, designed to be referenced bedside or in a clinic encounter.

## Why it's apex

The 2022 HFrEF algorithm is the field's clearest example of *evidence compression as the artifact*. It takes ~200 pages of systematic reviews, randomised-trial summaries, and expert-consensus discussion and renders the actionable distillation as a single navigable image. Four moves earn the apex rating: (1) **the four-pillar framing** is the artifact's central innovation — the previous era's "sequential addition of therapies as tolerated" gave way to "all four initiated within four to six weeks," and the algorithm visualises the simultaneity rather than implying sequence; (2) **the Class × LoE annotation discipline** carries the evidence weight at every recommendation node, so a clinician reading the algorithm can see at a glance which arrows are Class I / LoE A (settled) vs Class IIa / LoE B-R (strong-but-second-line) vs Class IIb / LoE C-LD (consider-with-caution); (3) **the algorithm-box compression** of a 200-page guideline into a one-page image is the load-bearing pedagogical act — the page that a hospitalist actually consults at 6 a.m. before rounds is this page, not the full document; (4) **the bidirectional escalation/de-escalation arrows** make the algorithm a *living management tool* rather than a one-time initiation pathway — the clinician returning at six months can use the same artifact to up-titrate, down-titrate, or substitute based on tolerance.

## Decomposition

### 1. The four-pillar framing is the central design move

Pre-2022 guidelines presented HFrEF therapy as a sequential ladder: ACE inhibitor first, beta-blocker second, MRA third in selected patients, SGLT2 inhibitor as a newer addition. The 2022 algorithm explicitly rejects sequential framing and presents the four classes as a *simultaneous quartet* — initiate all four within four to six weeks, titrate each independently, and treat any class not at target as a deficit to be addressed. The visual move is critical: the four classes are rendered side-by-side, equal in box size, with the initiation arrow pointing to all four together. The clinician reading the algorithm cannot interpret it as "add the second pillar only after the first is at target" — the visual layout refuses that reading. The simultaneity framing comes from the post-2020 evidence on early initiation (PIONEER-HF for ARNI, EMPEROR-Reduced and DAPA-HF for SGLT2 inhibitors) and represents a substantive shift in management philosophy that the visual rendering enforces.

### 2. The Class of Recommendation system

Every action node carries one of four Class labels:
- **Class I** (strong; benefit >>> risk) — "is recommended," "should be performed"
- **Class IIa** (moderate; benefit >> risk) — "is reasonable," "can be useful"
- **Class IIb** (weak; benefit ≥ risk) — "may be reasonable," "might be considered"
- **Class III** (no benefit OR harm; risk ≥ or > benefit) — "is not recommended," "should not be performed"

The Class label encodes the strength of the recommending committee's confidence in the action. The clinician reading the algorithm decodes Class before reading the recommendation text. The discipline is the same as the typographic discipline of color-coding in atlases (Netter's vascular colors, AO's anatomy conventions): the formal annotation carries the cognitive weight, and the prose elaborates. A Class I recommendation in the algorithm is treated as binding for institutional quality metrics; a Class IIb is treated as a clinician-judgment node. The encoding makes that distinction immediately visible.

### 3. The Level of Evidence system

Adjacent to each Class label is a Level of Evidence:
- **A** — high-quality evidence from >1 RCT, or meta-analyses of high-quality RCTs
- **B-R** — moderate-quality evidence from >1 RCT (Randomised)
- **B-NR** — moderate-quality evidence from >1 well-designed non-randomised study, observational study, or registry (Non-Randomised)
- **C-LD** — randomised or non-randomised observational or registry studies with limitations of design or execution, meta-analyses of such studies, physiological or mechanistic studies (Limited Data)
- **C-EO** — consensus of expert opinion based on clinical experience (Expert Opinion)

The Class × LoE matrix is the central evidence-encoding system of the entire ACC/AHA guideline corpus. A clinician reading the algorithm sees, for example, that ARNI initiation in HFrEF is Class I, LoE A (strongest possible recommendation, highest-quality evidence) while inotrope use as a bridge to advanced therapy is Class IIb, LoE B-NR (weak recommendation, moderate-quality non-randomised evidence). The two-axis annotation is *more informative* than a single overall grade (the GRADE system collapses to one grade per recommendation, which the ACC/AHA system explicitly rejects). The discipline is part of why the ACC/AHA guidelines are the global reference for cardiovascular practice — the two-axis annotation respects the cognitive distinction between "how confident is the field" (LoE) and "how strongly do we recommend the action" (Class).

### 4. The one-page compression of 200 pages

The full 2022 HF Guideline runs ~200 pages; the HFrEF algorithm is one page. The compression ratio is ~200:1, and the algorithm carries the actionable distillation in a form the bedside clinician can use without retrieving the full guideline. The compression is enabled by the Class × LoE annotation system — the algorithm does not need to recapitulate the evidence in prose because the annotations carry the evidence summary at each node. The discipline parallels the relationship between an NIH R01 Specific Aims page and its 12-page Research Strategy (Wave 13 batch, NIH R01 tear-down): the compressed leading artifact is what the audience actually reads; the long-form document is the receipts. The clinician trusts the algorithm because the underlying 200 pages exist; the underlying 200 pages have authority because the algorithm distills them faithfully. The two-document architecture is the form's apex.

### 5. The bidirectional escalation/de-escalation arrows

Most pre-2022 treatment algorithms were *initiation pathways* — they showed how to start therapy and were silent on what to do at six months or one year. The 2022 HFrEF algorithm includes explicit *de-escalation* arrows: if the patient develops symptomatic hypotension on the ARNI, downward titration is shown; if creatinine rises >30% on the MRA, dose reduction or discontinuation is shown; if the patient develops bradycardia on the beta-blocker, the algorithm shows the threshold for dose reduction. The bidirectionality converts the algorithm from a one-time initiation tool into a *continuous management tool*. The clinician at any encounter — initial diagnosis, six-month follow-up, hospitalisation for decompensation — can use the same algorithm. The form's longevity in the clinician's workflow is the discipline's payoff.

### 6. Starter dose and target dose explicitly

Each pillar's recommendation includes both a *starter dose* (typically the lowest available dose, calibrated to be tolerated by the most patients) and a *target dose* (the dose at which trial-derived mortality benefit was demonstrated). For sacubitril/valsartan (the ARNI): start 24/26 mg twice daily (or 49/51 mg twice daily if the patient was on a moderate-dose ACE inhibitor or ARB previously), target 97/103 mg twice daily. For carvedilol (a beta-blocker): start 3.125 mg twice daily, target 25 mg twice daily (or 50 mg twice daily for patients > 85 kg). The explicit dose specification means the algorithm is *prescribable* — the clinician can write the order without consulting a separate dosing reference. The discipline parallels AO Surgery Reference's implant-specification routing (Wave 13 batch, AO tear-down): the artifact ends at the actionable specification, not at the device-or-drug class.

### 7. The "if cannot tolerate X then" contingencies

Each pillar has contingency branches for the common intolerances. *If* the patient cannot tolerate the ARNI (history of angioedema, severe hyperkalemia, severe renal impairment) *then* substitute an ACE inhibitor or ARB. *If* the patient cannot tolerate the MRA (severe hyperkalemia, eGFR < 30) *then* the pillar is omitted with documentation. *If* the patient cannot tolerate the beta-blocker (severe asthma, severe bradycardia with pacemaker contraindication) *then* the pillar is omitted with documentation. The contingencies are structured as IF/THEN pairs, the same structure I-PASS uses for handoff contingency planning (Wave 13 batch, I-PASS tear-down). The structural parallel is not accidental — both artifacts are operational tools for clinicians under time pressure, and the IF/THEN structure is the cognitive offloading discipline that makes them deployable.

### 8. The integration of comorbidity considerations

The algorithm includes adjacent boxes for the most common HFrEF-relevant comorbidities: hypertension, atrial fibrillation, iron deficiency (intravenous iron has Class I, LoE B-R for symptomatic relief in iron-deficient HFrEF), diabetes (SGLT2 inhibitors are a single agent serving both heart failure and diabetes goals), coronary artery disease (revascularisation in selected patients), and advanced therapy referral (cardiac resynchronisation, ICD, transplant evaluation). The comorbidity integration prevents the failure mode of treating heart failure in isolation while missing the comorbid management. The artifact's scope is *the patient's cardiovascular management*, not "the HFrEF prescription," and the visual layout enforces this by giving the comorbidity considerations equal visual weight to the four pillars.

### 9. The algorithm references the underlying recommendation tables by number

Each node in the algorithm carries a small recommendation-table reference (e.g., "see Table 9, Rec 4.2"). The reference is the bridge back to the full guideline: a clinician who wants to see the evidence underlying a Class IIa recommendation can navigate from the algorithm to the relevant recommendation table in two clicks (in the digital version) or one page-flip (in the print version). The reference discipline preserves the algorithm's compression while keeping the evidence accessible. The pattern is the same as Stripe Docs' three-column layout (tear-down #09) where the prose carries the explanation and the right column carries the reference: separation of *what to do* from *why* without losing access to *why*.

### 10. The visual hierarchy enforces the cognitive priority

The four pillars are rendered at the top of the algorithm, in large boxes, with prominent Class I annotations. The contingency branches are smaller, lower in the visual hierarchy, with their Class IIa or IIb annotations. The comorbidity considerations are smaller still, off to the side. The visual hierarchy matches the cognitive priority: the four pillars *must* be addressed in every HFrEF patient; the contingencies *may* apply; the comorbidity considerations *should be considered*. A clinician scanning the algorithm under time pressure decodes the priority from the visual layout before reading any annotation. The discipline is the same as Hoppenfeld's chapter-end synthesis (Wave 13 batch, Hoppenfeld tear-down): the visual integration encodes the priority for the reader.

### 11. The algorithm is updated, with version history visible

The ACC/AHA guideline corpus has a clear version history: the 2013 HF Guideline, the 2017 Focused Update, the 2022 full revision. Each iteration revises specific recommendations based on new evidence (the 2022 revision added SGLT2 inhibitors as the fourth pillar based on EMPEROR-Reduced and DAPA-HF; the 2017 focused update added the ARNI based on PARADIGM-HF). The version history is visible: the algorithm is dated, and the changes from prior versions are summarised in the guideline's "What's New" section. The version discipline parallels AO Surgery Reference's last-updated dates (Wave 13 batch, AO tear-down) and is what converts the artifact from a snapshot into a living document. A clinician trained on the 2017 algorithm needs to know what changed in 2022; the explicit version history makes the change auditable.

### 12. The guideline-as-artifact carries regulatory weight

ACC/AHA Class I recommendations are commonly adopted as institutional quality metrics (CMS, NCQA, Joint Commission). The algorithm's Class I nodes — "ARNI is recommended for symptomatic HFrEF," "MRA is recommended for HFrEF with eGFR > 30 and K < 5.0" — appear in hospital quality dashboards and are tracked as performance indicators. The regulatory weight comes from the guideline's process: the writing committee includes representatives from multiple stakeholder organisations, the evidence review follows the Institute of Medicine's standards for trustworthy guidelines, and the recommendations are peer-reviewed by external panels. The discipline matters because clinical algorithms without rigorous process can be adopted as quality metrics inappropriately, producing measurement-without-evidence. The ACC/AHA process is the gold standard the algorithm's authority rests on; borrowing the algorithm format without the process produces a less authoritative artifact.

## What it teaches

**For decision-walking artifacts under time pressure (maps to playbook `clinical-algorithm.md`).** The Class × LoE annotation discipline, the one-page compression, and the bidirectional arrows are portable to any decision-tree artifact where the user needs to act quickly with confidence-calibrated guidance. Our `clinical-algorithm.md` playbook (Wave 10) codifies these moves; the same disciplines transfer to incident-response runbooks, software-deployment decision trees, security-triage workflows.

**For evidence-weighted recommendation systems (maps to wow-taxonomy 2.4 provenance transparency, 2.10 the honest null).** The Class × LoE two-axis annotation is the apex pattern for embedding evidence weight in recommendations. The discipline is portable to any artifact that recommends actions based on evidence of varying quality: API documentation that distinguishes battle-tested patterns from experimental ones, security guidance that distinguishes settled best-practice from emerging consensus.

**For multi-component simultaneous initiation (vs. sequential addition).** The four-pillar framing's central design move — visualise simultaneity to enforce simultaneous initiation, where the sequential layout would imply sequential addition — is portable to any artifact whose effect depends on the user adopting *all components* rather than starting with one and adding others as tolerated. Onboarding flows, training programs, multi-step compliance requirements can all use this discipline.

**For the algorithm-and-evidence-document pair (maps to playbook `grant-application.md`, the Aims-page-plus-Research-Strategy structure).** The 200:1 compression ratio between the full guideline and the algorithm is the apex example of *compressed-leading-artifact + long-form-receipts*. The pattern is the cardiology analogue of the NIH R01 Aims page (Wave 13 batch). Borrow this two-document architecture for any artifact pair where the compressed version carries authority precisely because the long-form version exists.

**For the explicit-version-history discipline.** Living documents in evolving evidence domains benefit from visible version history. The discipline transfers to software documentation (release notes; last-updated dates), regulatory guidance (effective-date stamps; revision history), educational artifacts (curriculum-version markers).

## What we'd steal

**The Class × LoE annotation discipline at every recommendation node.** Our `clinical-algorithm.md` playbook requires both annotations as hard gates; the `templates/react-clinical-algorithm.tsx` template implements per-terminal Class + LoE banners. The discipline came from this guideline corpus.

**The four-pillar visual framing for simultaneous-initiation recommendations.** When the artifact's job is to enforce all-of-the-above adoption (rather than sequential consideration), render the components as equal-weight siblings rather than as a ladder. The visual move enforces the cognitive move.

**The bidirectional escalation/de-escalation arrows.** Make the algorithm a continuous management tool, not a one-time initiation pathway. Our `templates/react-clinical-algorithm.tsx` includes breadcrumb back-paths that approximate this; the next iteration should add explicit de-escalation branches.

**The starter-dose + target-dose explicit specification.** End the algorithm at prescribable specifications, not at device-or-drug-class generalities. Our medical templates that include dose recommendations enforce this; the discipline comes from this guideline.

**The IF/THEN contingency structure inside the algorithm.** Borrow the same structure I-PASS uses for handoff contingencies. The cognitive offloading is the same; the structural parallel reinforces the cross-artifact discipline.

## What we wouldn't copy

**The Class × LoE annotation system has been criticised for inter-rater variability.** Multiple studies have shown that guideline panels assigning Class × LoE to the same evidence can disagree (Tricoci et al. *JAMA* 2009 — a substantial fraction of ACC/AHA recommendations were Class C, based on expert opinion rather than evidence). The annotation system is the field's best available compromise but is not above critique. Borrow the discipline of evidence-weighting; do not assume the Class × LoE system itself is settled.

**The four-pillar framing is HFrEF-specific.** The simultaneous-initiation discipline arose from the specific evidence base for HFrEF therapy; it is not transferrable unchanged to HFpEF (heart failure with preserved EF, where the evidence base is thinner and the therapy more individualised), to heart-rhythm management (where sequential addition is sometimes correct), or to other cardiovascular conditions. The visual move is portable; the simultaneous-initiation framing is condition-specific.

**The guideline-as-quality-metric coupling has produced perverse incentives in some institutional settings.** When Class I recommendations are tracked as quality metrics, the institutional pressure to maximise compliance can override individual clinician judgment, including in patients where the recommendation is contraindicated. The discipline of clinical-recommendation-as-quality-metric is a separate question from the discipline of algorithm design; borrow the algorithm's design without importing the regulatory-coupling assumptions.

## Cross-references

- **Playbook:** `references/medium-playbooks/clinical-algorithm.md` (Wave 10; the playbook this tear-down anchors)
- **Sibling playbook:** `references/medium-playbooks/clinical-pathway-stepwise.md` (Wave 11; the stepwise-pathway sibling, used by ACC/AHA for staged-treatment recommendations)
- **Templates:** `templates/mermaid-clinical-algorithm.mmd` (Surviving Sepsis Hour-1 Bundle with full ANSI shape vocabulary + ACC/AHA classDef); `templates/react-clinical-algorithm.tsx` (HEART pathway chest-pain decision-walker with useReducer state machine; per-terminal Class + LoE banner); `templates/clinical-pathway-stepwise.md` (GINA 2024 asthma 5-step ladder as the stepwise-pathway parallel)
- **Library:** `references/libraries/medical-artifacts.md` §21 (guidelines as designed artifacts); §22 (recommendation-annotation systems including ACC/AHA, GRADE, GINA, NICE)
- **Wow-taxonomy moves:** 2.4 provenance transparency (Class × LoE encodes evidence weight); 2.10 the honest null (Class III recommendations against ineffective therapies); 2.7 data-ink ratio approaches 1.0 (one-page compression of 200 pages); B.1 signature progression (ACC/AHA guideline corpus across decades); 3.12 section-as-control (the algorithm IS the control surface for management decisions)
- **Adjacent tear-downs:** `nih-r01-specific-aims.md` (this Wave 13 batch; shares the compressed-leading-artifact + long-form-receipts pattern); `i-pass-handoff.md` (this Wave 13 batch; shares the IF/THEN contingency structure); `who-surgical-safety-checklist.md` (this Wave 13 batch; shares the one-page actionable distillation of a deeper evidence base); `uptodate-algorithm-box.md` (this Wave 13 batch; the clinical-reference-platform sibling)

# Tear-Down: AO Surgery Reference (aosurgery.org)

**Publisher:** AO Foundation (Arbeitsgemeinschaft für Osteosynthesefragen — Association for the Study of Internal Fixation), Davos, Switzerland; founded 1958 by Maurice E. Müller, Robert Schneider, Hans Willenegger, Martin Allgöwer
**First online release:** 2003; current iteration continuously updated
**URL:** surgeryreference.aofoundation.org (legacy: aosurgery.org)
**Coverage:** all major fracture types and reconstructive procedures across orthopaedic trauma, craniomaxillofacial surgery, spine, hand, veterinary, and pediatric subspecialties; >5,000 procedures with step-by-step illustrated technique
**Where to study:** the site itself (free for selected modules; institutional access for full library); the print companion *AO Principles of Fracture Management* (Rüedi, Buckley, Moran, 3rd ed., Thieme 2017); the AO Trauma Curriculum that anchors the orthopaedic residency in much of Europe and increasingly in North America

## What it is

The AO Surgery Reference is a free-to-clinicians online surgical atlas covering virtually every fracture treatment and reconstructive procedure performed by orthopaedic, maxillofacial, and spine surgeons. Each procedure is presented as a numbered step-by-step technique: every step has an *anatomy panel* showing the relevant surrounding structures, a *surgical-field panel* showing what the operator sees at that step (or a perspective rendering when an operator's-eye view is impossible), and a *dictation caption* describing what is happening in the language of the operative note. The site classifies fractures using the AO/OTA system (a Müller-derived alphanumeric taxonomy adopted by the *Journal of Orthopaedic Trauma* as the standard reporting nomenclature), shows fluoroscopy and radiograph integration at each substantive step, names structures at risk for every step, and routes the user from the chosen approach to the appropriate implant specification, including manufacturer-specific options when relevant. The whole reference is a *teaching atlas at the resolution of a step-by-step operative note*.

## Why it's apex

The AO Surgery Reference is the field's gold-standard digital surgical atlas, and the reason is *the discipline of pairing*. Every step has two synchronised panels (anatomy + surgical field) plus a caption written in operative-note voice; every fracture type has an alternative-approaches table at the top before the chosen approach is taught; every step names structures at risk by *name* (the lateral cutaneous nerve of the thigh, the profunda femoris artery), not by category ("the neurovascular bundle"). The pairing discipline is what makes the artifact survive the operating room: the trainee, scrubbed, reading from a tablet between cases, can match what they see in the anatomy panel to what is hidden by retractors in the field. Four moves earn the apex rating: (1) **the anatomy panel is the safety scaffold**, restated at every step so the trainee never loses the anatomic frame; (2) **the alternative-approaches table at the top of every fracture acknowledges the surgeon's choice space**, refusing the textbook habit of teaching one approach as if it were the only one; (3) **the implant-specification routing closes the loop** to the actual hardware on the back table; (4) **the dictation caption** teaches the trainee how to dictate the operative note while teaching them the procedure, collapsing two pedagogies into one panel.

## Decomposition

### 1. The synchronised two-panel composition is the form's signature

Every numbered step has two panels rendered side-by-side: an *anatomy panel* and a *surgical-field panel*. The anatomy panel is a clean line illustration (in the Brödel tradition; Max Brödel founded the modern medical-illustration discipline at Johns Hopkins in 1911) showing the relevant deep structures *as if soft-tissue layers above were transparent*. The surgical-field panel shows what the surgeon's eye actually sees at the same moment — retractors in place, dermal edges retracted, fascia opened. The two panels are *spatially registered*: the anatomic structure named in the anatomy panel is at the same screen position as the corresponding region in the surgical-field panel. The discipline lets the reader's eye move horizontally between the two panels without re-orienting. This is the move textbook atlases (Campbell's, Rockwood) approximate with side-by-side figures and that AO Surgery Reference systematises across every step of every procedure.

### 2. The dictation caption teaches operative-note voice alongside technique

Every step's caption is written in the voice of the operative note: short imperative sentences, second-person elided, present-progressive verbs, anatomic landmarks named in standard nomenclature. "The fascia lata is incised in line with the skin incision and reflected laterally. The vastus lateralis is identified and elevated from the intermuscular septum, exposing the femur." The caption is not "the surgeon now elevates the vastus lateralis" (textbook voice) and is not "I elevated the vastus lateralis" (first-person voice). It is the voice the trainee will be expected to use in their own operative notes, and reading two thousand AO captions trains that voice. The pedagogy is double: the trainee learns the surgical technique *and* the documentation discipline in the same panel.

### 3. The alternative-approaches table at the top of every fracture type

Before any specific approach is taught, the fracture-type page shows a table of the approaches available, with one-line summaries of when each is indicated. For a proximal humerus fracture: deltopectoral / extended deltopectoral / anterolateral acromial / superior. For a hip fracture requiring open reduction: Smith-Petersen / Watson-Jones / Hardinge / posterior. The table forces the trainee to *see the choice space* before being taught one option. The discipline is anti-dogmatic — there is no AO Surgery Reference page that teaches the deltopectoral approach as if it were the only valid approach to the proximal humerus. The pedagogical cost (the trainee has to read more before learning the procedure) is paid in surgical maturity: trainees who learn anatomy this way are better prepared to choose approaches in unfamiliar cases.

### 4. Structures at risk are named per step, not as a chapter-opening warning

Most surgical textbooks list structures at risk for a procedure once, at the beginning of the chapter, as a warning. AO Surgery Reference repeats the at-risk structures *for each step where they are at risk*. Step 4 of the posterior approach to the hip: "structures at risk: sciatic nerve (medial to the short external rotators)." Step 6, after the rotators are released: "structures at risk: sciatic nerve (now in the operative field, retracted medially with the posterior capsule)." The repetition is not redundancy — it is *step-specific spatial reminder*. The trainee reading the chapter once retains the warning at the moment in the procedure where it applies, not as a generalised anxiety throughout.

### 5. The AO/OTA classification depth

Every fracture is classified using the Müller-derived AO/OTA system: a bone-region-type-group-subgroup alphanumeric (e.g., 31-A2.2 for a pertrochanteric femur fracture with an intermediate fragment). The reference does not just *cite* the classification — it teaches it. Each fracture-type page shows the type, group, and subgroup definitions, with example radiographs at each level. The discipline forces the trainee to *categorise* the fracture before choosing the treatment, which is the discipline the orthopaedic exam (OITE, ABOS) tests and which clinical research uses as the standard reporting nomenclature. Borrowing the AO/OTA system into our system's templates (`templates/svg-radiograph-overlay.svg` uses 31-A2.2 specifically) is the right move — the standard exists; use it; teach with it.

### 6. The radiograph and fluoroscopy integration is per-step, not chapter-end

Operative-imaging guidance (intra-operative fluoroscopy positioning, expected radiographic appearance after the step, common malreductions visible on imaging) is integrated *into the step that uses it*, not collected at the chapter end. Step 8 of intramedullary nailing of the femur: the panel includes a fluoroscopy still showing the expected start-point position with the entry reamer in place, plus a labeled diagram showing what the start point should *not* look like (too lateral, too anterior). The discipline matches the operative reality — the surgeon checks fluoroscopy at the moment they need it, not at the end of the case — and gives the trainee a pattern-recognition library tied to procedural steps.

### 7. The implant-specification routing closes to actual hardware

Each procedure terminates in an implant-selection panel: for a 31-A2.2 fracture in a 75-year-old, the page routes the trainee to the cephalomedullary nail family (e.g., Synthes TFNA, Stryker Gamma3, Zimmer Natural Nail), shows the available lengths and diameters, and links to the specific implant template overlays for pre-operative planning. The discipline closes the loop from *teaching the technique* to *executing the case on the back table*. The trainee who reads the AO page the night before a case can request the implant tray by specific name. Adjacent atlases (Campbell's, Skeletal Trauma) describe procedures in implant-neutral language; AO Surgery Reference is implant-specific, which is more useful for case preparation and more vulnerable to industry-relationship critique (see "What we wouldn't copy" below).

### 8. The procedure index is by approach AND by fracture

The site offers two parallel navigation paths: by anatomic region (proximal femur → pertrochanteric → cephalomedullary nailing) and by surgical approach (posterior approach to the hip → applications: THA, hemiarthroplasty, posterior column fixation). Either path arrives at the same procedure pages, but the navigation choice determines the reader's mental frame. A resident the night before a case enters from "what am I doing tomorrow?" (anatomic region); a chief preparing a teaching conference enters from "what approaches should the junior residents know?" (approach). The dual navigation matches actual clinical workflow.

### 9. The illustration style is unified across decades and authors

AO Surgery Reference has been drawn by multiple medical illustrators across two decades (Jecca Reichmuth, Sandra Gonser, others associated with the AO Foundation's Davos studio), but the visual style is rigorously unified: the same line weight, the same anatomic color convention (red arteries, blue veins, yellow nerves — the Netter convention adapted for line illustration), the same composition discipline (anatomy panel always shows the *contralateral* side reflected when needed for clarity, surgical-field panel always from the operating surgeon's eye position). The visual coherence across thousands of figures is the artifact-as-series discipline that Netter achieved as a single artist and that AO has achieved through editorial standards across a studio.

### 10. The dual web-and-print existence

The AO Surgery Reference shipped as a web-first product but has print companions (the *AO Principles* book series). The web version supports interactive 3D anatomy models, zoomable radiographs, and procedure-specific videos; the print companion uses the same illustrations and captions in a page-bound form for reading without screens. The two-format strategy serves different reader modes (case-night prep is web; long-form learning is print) without producing two divergent artifacts — the print is *literally the web pages in book form*. The discipline matches Stripe Press's web-to-print continuity (see tear-down #25).

### 11. The free-tier strategy is a form-spreading move

The AO Foundation publishes much of the Surgery Reference free to clinicians worldwide. The strategic logic resembles the I-PASS Institute's: the artifact's adoption depends on its accessibility, and proprietary surgical atlases (industry-published technique manuals, paywalled textbook chapters) have not displaced AO Surgery Reference even with marketing budgets. The free tier is a portability move — a resident in Mumbai, Lagos, or Lima can use the artifact as readily as a resident in Boston, and the worldwide consistency of orthopaedic technique in AO-trained surgeons is the artifact's effect.

### 12. The artifact is updated, with version history visible

Procedures are revised as evidence accumulates: a 2012 intramedullary nail technique page differs from the 2024 version in the reaming-vs-non-reaming guidance, the entry-point recommendation for newer nail designs, the implant family options. The reference shows a "last updated" date on every page, and substantive revisions are versioned. The discipline matches what UpToDate does for medical knowledge (see tear-down #8 in this Wave 13 batch); the absence of versioning would convert the reference into a snapshot rather than a living document. The version discipline costs editorial capacity; the AO Foundation maintains a permanent editorial staff to do this work, which most academic societies cannot.

## What it teaches

**For technical and surgical illustration (maps to wow-taxonomy 1.11 calligraphic-stroke register, 2.7 data-ink ratio approaches 1.0).** The anatomy-plus-surgical-field paired-panel composition is portable to any domain where the reader needs to map an idealised mental model to the messier reality. Engineering instruction (the schematic alongside the actual circuit board), automotive repair (the service-manual diagram alongside the engine-bay photograph), even cooking (the recipe diagram alongside the in-pan photo) — the pattern transfers.

**For documentation that closes to action (maps to playbook `surgical-technique.md`).** The implant-specification routing — the reference that ends at "request this specific part" rather than at "such-and-such device family" — is portable to any technical documentation that prepares the reader for execution. API documentation that ends at "call this endpoint with these parameters" beats documentation that ends at "you may want to consider the relevant endpoints in this family."

**For step-specific safety reminders (maps to taxonomy 7.5 minimum-difference example set).** The structures-at-risk-per-step discipline is the surgical analogue of the minimum-difference example set in education: each step's at-risk list differs from the previous step's by one or two elements, surfacing exactly what changes as the operation progresses. Borrow this pattern any time a multi-step procedure has different risks at different steps; the per-step reminder is more useful than the chapter-opening warning.

**For artifact-as-series consistency (maps to wow-taxonomy B.1 signature progression).** Visual coherence across thousands of figures, drawn by multiple illustrators over two decades, is achieved through editorial discipline rather than single-author discipline. The AO model is portable to any team-produced visual artifact (technical documentation, training materials, scientific figures across a lab's papers) — the editorial standards document is the artifact behind the artifact.

**For the choice-space-before-choice discipline.** The alternative-approaches table at the top of every fracture type is a pedagogical move that teaches the reader to think about the choice space before being taught one option. Borrow this pattern in any educational artifact: list the alternatives, briefly justify the one being taught, then teach it. The discipline produces readers who can adapt outside the teaching frame.

## What we'd steal

**The synchronised two-panel composition.** Every step gets an idealised representation paired with a real-execution representation. Our `templates/surgical-technique.md` template attempts this with anatomy + intra-op + dictation caption per step; the AO Surgery Reference is the apex implementation to calibrate against.

**The structures-at-risk-per-step discipline.** Repeat the safety reminders at every step where they apply, not once at the chapter opening. Our `surgical-technique.md` playbook requires structures-at-risk-per-step as a hard gate; the requirement comes from AO.

**The alternative-approaches table convention.** Open every procedure page with a one-line summary of the alternative approaches before teaching the chosen one. Our template's "approaches table" section comes from this; the discipline transfers to any multi-option technical decision.

**The implant-specification routing.** End procedures at the actual hardware, not at the device family. The template's "implant specification" section enforces this; for non-surgical artifacts, the same discipline applies to *parts lists*, *bill of materials*, *exact endpoint names*.

**The dictation caption voice.** Captions written in the voice the trainee will need to produce themselves. Borrow this pattern in any artifact where the trainee will eventually have to write in the same voice — code documentation written in the comment voice the trainee should learn, project documentation written in the change-log voice they will produce.

## What we wouldn't copy

**The implant specificity has industry-relationship complications.** AO Foundation has historical and contemporary ties to Synthes (now DePuy Synthes / Johnson & Johnson) and other implant manufacturers; the reference's tendency to name specific products is partly an artifact of those relationships. Adopting implant-specificity in our system without that relationship structure is fine; importing the relationship structure to enable it is not. The discipline of routing to specific parts is good; the dependency on the manufacturer is bad.

**The editorial-staff capacity is enormous.** The AO Foundation maintains a permanent editorial staff, a studio of medical illustrators, and a clinical advisory board across multiple subspecialties. Most institutions cannot replicate this. Borrowing the editorial discipline (versioning, last-updated dates, alternative-approaches tables) is portable; assuming the institutional capacity to enforce them across a comparable corpus is not. Start with the discipline at the scale you can sustain.

**The web-product UX has dated as the web has matured.** The site's navigation and search remain functional but have not kept pace with contemporary web UX patterns (Stripe Docs, Linear, contemporary documentation sites). The artifact's core craft (illustrations, captions, classification) is apex; the surrounding web product is closer to ship-acceptable. Borrow the substance; do not borrow the chrome.

## Cross-references

- **Playbook:** `references/medium-playbooks/surgical-technique.md` (Wave 11; the playbook this tear-down anchors)
- **Sibling playbook:** `references/medium-playbooks/physical-exam-maneuver.md` (the per-step pairing discipline transfers to physical-exam technique)

- **Adjacent tear-downs:** `#17-netter-atlas-plate.md` (the line-illustration tradition AO extends to surgical context); `#32-vesalius-fabrica.md` (the founding anatomical-illustration tradition); `#09-stripe-docs.md` (technical documentation done at apex)

# Tear-Down: Stanley Hoppenfeld — *Physical Examination of the Spine and Extremities* (1976)

**Author:** Stanley Hoppenfeld, MD (1935-2020), Albert Einstein College of Medicine / Montefiore
**First published:** Appleton-Century-Crofts, 1976; Pearson/Prentice Hall later editions; still in print as the field's reference text
**Illustrator:** Hugh Thomas (line drawings throughout); a single-artist atlas
**Companion:** *Surgical Exposures in Orthopaedics: The Anatomic Approach* (Hoppenfeld and de Boer, 1984) — applies the same discipline to surgical approaches
**Audience:** orthopaedic residents (PGY-1 through PGY-3), sports-medicine fellows, primary-care physicians learning the musculoskeletal exam, physical therapists, athletic trainers
**Where to study:** the 1976 first edition (used copies; the form is unchanged in later editions); Magee's *Orthopedic Physical Assessment* (Saunders, 7th ed., 2021) as the comprehensive contemporary heir; Hattam and Smeatham's *Special Tests in Musculoskeletal Examination* as the per-test focused descendant

## What it is

A 280-page line-illustrated atlas of the musculoskeletal physical examination, organised by anatomic region (cervical spine, shoulder, elbow, wrist and hand, thoracic and lumbar spine, hip, knee, ankle and foot, gait). Each chapter follows an unvarying structure: surface anatomy → bony anatomy → muscle and ligament anatomy → neurovascular anatomy → inspection → palpation → range of motion → muscle testing → neurologic exam → provocative tests → special tests → considerations from adjacent regions. The book teaches the *anatomy first*, then the *exam second*, with the discipline that no provocative test is presented before its anatomic substrate has been taught. Every test gets a three-panel illustration: patient positioning, the maneuver itself, and the interpretation. The book has remained the orthopaedic field's reference for nearly fifty years; many residents own a hardcover copy that has survived three residencies and two fellowships.

## Why it's apex

Hoppenfeld is the apex example of *anatomy-first physical-exam pedagogy*, and the reason the discipline works is the radical refusal of photography. The book uses only line drawings, never photographs, and the choice is load-bearing: line drawings *abstract* away the patient's particularity (skin tone, body habitus, the specific anatomic variation of the model) and force the reader to attend to the *generalisable structure* of the test. Four moves explain the apex rating: (1) **the anatomy-before-test discipline** opens every chapter with the relevant surface, bony, and neurovascular anatomy *before* any provocative test is introduced — the test is then a *consequence* of the anatomy, not a memorised maneuver; (2) **the triptych composition per test** (positioning, maneuver, interpretation) is invariant across hundreds of tests, becoming a cognitive frame the reader brings to any new test; (3) **the single-illustrator unity** across the entire book (Hugh Thomas drew all 1000+ figures) gives the artifact a visual coherence that multi-illustrator atlases never achieve; (4) **the composite-test logic** is taught explicitly — no single provocative test is presented as diagnostic alone; the reader learns to *combine* the Lachman, anterior drawer, and pivot shift for the ACL because no one test does the diagnostic work alone.

## Decomposition

### 1. Anatomy before test; the structural discipline is the form

Open any chapter in Hoppenfeld and the first 8-12 pages are *anatomy*: surface anatomy (where the structures are on the skin), bony anatomy (the underlying skeleton), muscular anatomy (the muscles that move the joint), ligamentous anatomy (the static stabilisers), neurovascular anatomy (the nerves and vessels that pass through). Only after this anatomic foundation does the chapter introduce the first physical-examination maneuver. The discipline is the form. A reader who has worked through the shoulder chapter's anatomy section can *reason* about why the empty-can test isolates the supraspinatus (the muscle's tendon runs under the acromion; arm position in 90° abduction with thumbs-down maximises supraspinatus contraction against a fulcrum). The reader who learned the empty-can test as a memorised maneuver cannot reason about its variants. The anatomy-first discipline is the difference between memorised technique and diagnostic thinking.

### 2. The triptych composition for every provocative test

Each provocative test is presented in three sequential panels:

- **Panel 1: Positioning** — the examiner and the patient in starting position; the limb at the correct angle; the examiner's hands at the correct contact points.
- **Panel 2: Maneuver** — the dynamic step; arrows showing the direction of force the examiner applies; the patient's expected motion or resistance.
- **Panel 3: Interpretation** — what a positive test looks like (often a small arrow showing pathological motion or an icon of the patient's pain response); what a negative test looks like.

The triptych is invariant across the book. A reader new to a chapter sees the format and immediately knows how to read it: positioning → maneuver → interpretation. The format is the cognitive frame the reader brings to any new test, including tests not in the book. The discipline is portable: any teaching artifact for a physical maneuver (the cardiac exam, the abdominal exam, the cranial nerve exam) can borrow the three-panel composition and get the same cognitive benefit. Our `templates/svg-lachman-test.svg` and `templates/react-lachman-test.tsx` implement this composition directly.

### 3. Line drawing as the medium, refused photography

Hugh Thomas's line drawings are the entire visual vocabulary; there is no photograph in the book. The choice is deliberate and load-bearing. Photographs of physical-exam maneuvers carry irrelevant information (the patient's clothing, the examination-room lighting, the model's skin tone, the examiner's specific hand size and skin tone). Line drawings *abstract*: they show the generalisable structure of the maneuver — the angles, the contact points, the direction of force — without the noise. Readers who learn the test from a line drawing can apply it to any patient body habitus; readers who learn from a photograph sometimes struggle to translate to a body habitus that differs from the model. The line-drawing discipline also has a representation-justice dimension: the abstraction removes the question of *whose body* is the canonical example, which photograph-based texts wrestle with (often by including diverse models, which solves part but not all of the problem). Hoppenfeld's solution — abstract entirely — is one valid answer; contemporary photograph-based texts make the opposite choice deliberately.

### 4. The sensitivity and specificity context per test

Each provocative test is presented with its sensitivity and specificity values, drawn from the orthopaedic-evidence literature current at the time of each edition. The Lachman test for ACL tear: Sn ~85%, Sp ~94%. The McMurray test for meniscal tear: Sn ~55%, Sp ~77%. The values matter because they teach the reader to *calibrate* their diagnostic confidence — a positive Lachman is high-specificity-grade evidence, a positive McMurray is moderate-specificity-grade. The discipline matches contemporary evidence-based-medicine pedagogy (EBM textbooks teach the same Sn/Sp calibration), but Hoppenfeld was teaching it in 1976 before EBM was formalised as a discipline. The book's evidence-orientation is precocious.

### 5. The composite-test logic: no single test is the diagnosis

Hoppenfeld explicitly teaches that no single provocative test is diagnostic. The ACL is assessed with Lachman + anterior drawer + pivot shift (three tests, three different mechanisms, three different Sn/Sp profiles); the rotator cuff with Neer + Hawkins + empty-can + Jobe drop-arm; the meniscus with McMurray + Apley + joint-line tenderness + Thessaly. The chapter on each region shows how the tests *combine* — which combinations are sensitive (rule-out clusters), which are specific (rule-in clusters), which redundancy is acceptable when one test cannot be performed (a patient with severe pain may not tolerate the pivot shift, so the Lachman becomes the load-bearing test). The composite-test logic is what separates the apex orthopaedic examiner from the rote-maneuver examiner. The book teaches this discipline explicitly.

### 6. The chapter-end synthesis: putting the exam together for the clinical encounter

Each chapter ends with a synthesis section: a worked example of the full musculoskeletal exam for the region, in the order the clinician would actually perform it (inspection while taking the history, palpation while explaining, range of motion as transition, then strength, then neurologic, then provocative). The synthesis is the discipline of *integrating* the components into a flow the patient experiences as one exam, not as a checklist of disconnected maneuvers. The patient-experience integration matters: a clinician who performs the exam as a checklist makes the patient feel like a case; a clinician who performs the exam as an integrated assessment makes the patient feel attended to. The teaching distinction is subtle and load-bearing for clinical work.

### 7. Cross-references to adjacent regions

The shoulder chapter cross-references the cervical spine exam (cervical radiculopathy can mimic shoulder pathology); the lumbar spine chapter cross-references the hip exam (hip pathology refers to the buttock and thigh, mimicking lumbar disease). The cross-references are explicit, with page numbers. The discipline teaches the reader that *the chief complaint is not always at the site of pathology* — referred pain is the rule, not the exception. The cross-reference structure is what converts the book from an atlas of isolated regional exams into an atlas of *clinical diagnostic reasoning* about musculoskeletal complaints.

### 8. Single-illustrator unity across decades

Hugh Thomas illustrated every figure in the original edition and contributed to subsequent editions before his death. The single-illustrator unity is what produces the book's visual coherence: the same line weight, the same anatomic conventions (consistent shading, consistent direction of force arrows, consistent stick-figure proportions for the examiner), the same level of stylisation across all 1000+ figures. The contrast with multi-illustrator orthopaedic atlases (Campbell's, Rockwood) is immediate: those books have visible style drift between chapters, which the reader's eye notices and which costs cognitive load. Hoppenfeld's coherence is the artifact-as-series discipline at the bound-book scale. Our `templates/react-lachman-test.tsx` borrows this discipline by enforcing the three-panel composition across all maneuvers in our system.

### 9. The deliberate gendering, then the correction

The 1976 edition's line drawings have a recognisable mid-1970s aesthetic and the examiner figures are predominantly male. Subsequent editions corrected this — examiner figures of multiple genders, more varied patient figures — without changing the underlying visual style. The correction matters for representation, and the *minimal-redraw* approach (change the examiner figure, preserve the technique illustration) is a portable lesson: representation-correction does not require redrawing the whole atlas, and may produce better outcomes than a wholesale visual restart. The lesson generalises to any long-running visual artifact undergoing demographic update.

### 10. The deliberate non-coverage

Hoppenfeld does *not* cover imaging interpretation, surgical management, or non-musculoskeletal causes of musculoskeletal symptoms. The omissions are named: the book is *the physical examination*, period. Imaging belongs in a radiology atlas; surgery belongs in *Surgical Exposures* (the companion volume); medical causes of musculoskeletal symptoms belong in an internal-medicine text. The discipline of *naming the scope* is part of the book's authority. A reader who needs imaging interpretation does not blame Hoppenfeld for not providing it; they consult a different book. The scope-naming is the form of intellectual honesty Pathoma's tear-down (#19) calls "deliberate sacrifices, named."

### 11. The book's longevity is a craft signal

*Physical Examination of the Spine and Extremities* has remained the field's reference for nearly fifty years. The longevity is a craft signal: subsequent texts (Magee, Konin, Hattam) cover more material, cite more evidence, include photographs, but none have displaced Hoppenfeld as *the* atlas residents own. The reason is the form: the anatomy-first discipline, the triptych composition, and the line-drawing abstraction produce an artifact that teaches *durably* — readers who learn the orthopaedic exam from Hoppenfeld retain the framework decades later. Newer texts cover more breadth at the cost of the framework, and the trade is wrong for an introductory atlas. The lesson: in form-stable domains (physical examination has not fundamentally changed since 1976), form-stable artifacts can outlive several generations of comprehensive updates.

### 12. The companion text completes the system

*Surgical Exposures in Orthopaedics: The Anatomic Approach* (Hoppenfeld and de Boer, 1984) is the surgical companion. It uses the same anatomy-first discipline, the same line-drawing style (Hugh Thomas again), the same step-by-step composition for surgical approaches. The two-book set is a *system*: the physical-exam atlas teaches the resident to *find* the pathology, the surgical-exposure atlas teaches the resident to *access* the pathology. The two-book design parallels Pathoma's book-plus-video pedagogy (#19) and AO Surgery Reference's anatomy-plus-surgical-field paired panels (this Wave 13 batch, AO tear-down): the artifact pair is more than the sum.

## What it teaches

**For physical-exam and procedure-instruction artifacts (maps to playbook `physical-exam-maneuver.md`).** The triptych composition (positioning + maneuver + interpretation) is the cognitive frame portable to any physical-skills instruction: cardiac auscultation, abdominal palpation, dental examination, ophthalmoscopy, athletic-trainer assessment. Our `physical-exam-maneuver.md` playbook codifies this; the discipline transfers beyond medicine to any teaching artifact for a learned-by-doing physical skill.

**For anatomy-first pedagogy (maps to wow-taxonomy 7.3 faded scaffold, 7.5 minimum-difference example set).** Teach the substrate before the maneuver. The discipline is the cousin of the worked-example fade: build the substrate, then introduce the technique as a *consequence* of the substrate, not as an isolated step. The artifact that teaches the maneuver before the anatomy produces brittle technical skill; the artifact that teaches the anatomy first produces durable diagnostic reasoning.

**For line drawing as abstraction (maps to wow-taxonomy 1.11 calligraphic-stroke register, 1.8 hand-crafted ornamentation at scale).** Line drawing abstracts away patient particularity and forces attention to the generalisable structure. The lesson is portable to any teaching artifact where the specific instance would carry irrelevant information: technical diagrams, architectural sections, mechanism illustrations. Photography is sometimes wrong even when it is available; the discipline of *choosing abstraction* is a craft move.

**For composite-test logic in diagnostic artifacts.** No single test is diagnostic. Teach the *combination* of tests, with their Sn/Sp profiles, as the diagnostic strategy. Portable to medical-decision artifacts, security-assessment tools, quality-assurance procedures — anywhere the diagnostic process is multi-test and the combination is what produces the answer.

**For single-author / single-illustrator coherence at scale (maps to wow-taxonomy B.1 signature progression).** Hugh Thomas's 1000+ figures across the book are the artifact-as-series discipline executed by one hand. The discipline is rare; when achievable, it produces visual coherence multi-author teams cannot match. Our system's templates and SVG illustrations should aim for similar coherence within the constraints of a multi-agent authoring process.

## What we'd steal

**The triptych composition for every physical-exam maneuver.** Three panels: positioning, maneuver, interpretation. Invariant across maneuvers. Our `templates/svg-lachman-test.svg` and `templates/react-lachman-test.tsx` already implement this; the discipline should be the default for any physical-exam artifact in our system.

**The anatomy-first chapter discipline.** Open the chapter with the relevant anatomy before introducing the maneuvers. The discipline is enforced in our `physical-exam-maneuver.md` playbook; templates should respect it.

**The composite-test framing.** Present provocative tests with their Sn/Sp values *and* the cluster of tests they participate in, not as isolated diagnostic maneuvers. Our `react-lachman-test.tsx` cites the Lachman's Sn/Sp; the next iteration should add the ACL-cluster framing (Lachman + anterior drawer + pivot shift).

**The line-drawing abstraction over photography.** When a teaching artifact needs to generalise across patient particularity, prefer line drawing. The discipline transfers to architectural section, mechanism diagram, schematic. Our SVG templates default to line drawing; the choice is the right one for the abstraction the artifact needs.

**The scope-naming discipline.** State explicitly what the artifact does *not* cover. The discipline is what Pathoma (#19) calls "deliberate sacrifices, named"; Hoppenfeld names them as scope boundaries. The lesson is the same: an artifact gains credibility from honest scope-naming, not from breadth-creep.

## What we wouldn't copy

**The exclusively-line-drawing choice is sometimes wrong.** For physical-exam maneuvers where the patient's specific anatomy is the diagnostic signal (dermatologic exam, ophthalmologic fundus exam, certain cardiac auscultation findings), photography or video carries information line drawing cannot. The discipline of choosing the medium is what matters; the choice of *always* line drawing is wrong for some artifacts. Tune the medium to the diagnostic signal.

**The single-illustrator model is not scalable to our system.** Our system has multiple agents, multiple illustrators, multiple template authors. The single-hand coherence Hoppenfeld achieves is impossible at our authoring model. The substitute is editorial discipline (style guides, template-enforced compositions, lint-checked visual standards) — the discipline AO Surgery Reference achieves at the studio scale. Borrow the editorial substitute; do not aspire to the single-hand original.

**The 1976 publication frame has dated in non-trivial ways.** The book's representational defaults (gender, body habitus, the assumption of a clinical encounter with an unhurried examination) reflect 1976 American orthopaedics. The corrections in subsequent editions help, but the underlying frame is of its era. Borrow the structural moves; do not borrow the frame.

## Cross-references

- **Playbook:** `references/medium-playbooks/physical-exam-maneuver.md` (Wave 11; the playbook this tear-down anchors)
- **Sibling playbook:** `references/medium-playbooks/surgical-technique.md` (the companion text *Surgical Exposures* is the surgical analogue)
- **Templates:** `templates/svg-lachman-test.svg` (Wave 10; Lachman test triptych in static SVG); `templates/react-lachman-test.tsx` (Wave 11; interactive Lachman with live flexion-angle and force sliders); `templates/react-fracture-classification.tsx` (Wave 11; the comparative-plate discipline applied to the Schatzker classification)
- **Library:** `references/libraries/medical-artifacts.md` §11 (physical-examination artifacts); `references/libraries/iconography-library.md` (line-drawing tradition)
- **Wow-taxonomy moves:** 1.11 calligraphic-stroke register (Hugh Thomas's line drawings); 7.3 faded scaffold (anatomy-first → maneuver-second pedagogy); 7.5 minimum-difference example set (composite-test clusters where each test differs from neighbors in one mechanism); B.1 signature progression (Thomas's 1000+ figures across decades); 2.7 data-ink ratio approaches 1.0 (line drawing's deliberate abstraction)
- **Adjacent tear-downs:** `#17-netter-atlas-plate.md` (the line-illustration tradition Hoppenfeld extends to physical-exam context); `#19-pathoma-condensed-review.md` (the deliberate-sacrifices-named discipline); `#32-vesalius-fabrica.md` (the founding anatomical-illustration tradition Hoppenfeld inherits)
